Hi, Doctor. Thanks for seeing me. I, um, I hope I'm not taking up a spot from someone who needs it more, but my wife really wanted me to come in. 

I guess the main thing is that I've just... I kind of stopped caring about everything I usually look forward to. I mean, I have a good life, I really do, so I feel a little guilty even complaining about it. But my fishing gear is just sitting untouched out in the garage, and... well, going out to the woodshop just feels like a chore lately. 

Even when my kids try to get me to play chess, I'm... I'm really just going through the motions. I feel terrible admitting that out loud, about my own kids. I just don't have the energy for it. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this okay for you.

I know I mentioned this a little bit already, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much... but yeah, I guess that's exactly it. I just... I don't get that same spark from things anymore.

Well, um... my sleep hasn't been the greatest, I suppose. I mean, I know everyone has trouble sleeping sometimes, especially when they're busy, so I'm sure it's nothing too out of the ordinary.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I mean, I know I shouldn't think this way, and I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself. I know I have a lot to be thankful for. But... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty awful about myself lately. 

It just feels like I'm letting everyone down. Like I've failed my wife by not being the rock she needs right now, and I'm failing the kids because I'm just going through the motions with them. And at work... I don't know, it's like every little thing that goes wrong on the site—if we go over budget on materials, or if someone misses a shift, or even just a minor safety hiccup—I just take it so personally. I keep thinking it's all my fault, like it's proof that I'm completely useless. 

Sometimes I even catch myself thinking that they'd all just be better off if I wasn't the one in charge anymore. I know that's probably a terrible thing to say, and I really should be stronger than this. I'm trying to push through it, I promise. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting to normal work stress.

Oh, um... I mean... I know that's a really important question, and I want to be completely honest with you. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or anything, or that I'm, you know, going to do something terrible. I know I have a family depending on me. 

But... well, sometimes, on those early drives to the site before anyone else is awake... it's just so quiet, and I'm so tired. I catch myself thinking that maybe it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. Like if something just happened. 

And sometimes... I hope this doesn't sound too awful, but sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me, because it feels a little too close, you know? Like the thought is just sitting right there. 

I would never want to put my family through that, I promise. I know it's a terrible way to think. I'm just so exhausted. Does that... I mean, is that something people with stress go through? I hope I'm not alarming you too much.

I hope I'm explaining this okay for you.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's been a little while, I guess. I kept thinking I could just snap out of it, you know? I didn't want to make a big deal out of nothing. But if I really think about it, it's been... probably about a year and seven months? Give or take. \n\nIt started right around the time my wife had some health problems, which kind of changed everything at home. I had to step up a lot more, which I was completely fine with! I mean, she's my wife, and I want to be there for her, so I really shouldn't be complaining. I just... I guess I haven't really bounced back to my normal self since then. \n\nI hope it's okay that I waited this long to come in. I probably should have handled it better and come to see you sooner, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any decrease in pleasure from activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I know I mentioned this a little bit already, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much... but yeah, I guess that's exactly it. I just... I don't get that same spark from things anymore. \n\nLike I said, my fishing gear is just gathering dust out there, and the woodshop... well, I used to love spending hours in there, but now I just look at it and feel tired. And the chess with the kids... I mean, they're great kids, they really are, and I feel awful saying this, but I'm just faking it for them. I'm not actually enjoying it. Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I know I need to keep my strength up for the crew and everything, so I really do try to eat. I don't want you to think I'm just starving myself on purpose or anything like that. 

But, uh, lately... I don't know, it's just like food doesn't really have any taste anymore? It's almost like chewing on cardboard most days. I hope I'm explaining this okay for you.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's been a little while, I guess. I kept thinking I could just snap out of it, you know? I didn't want to make a big deal out of nothing. But if I really think about it, it's been... probably about a year and seven months? Give or take. \n\nIt started right around the time my wife had some health problems, which kind of changed everything at home. I had to step up a lot more, which I was completely fine with! I mean, she's my wife, and I want to be there for her, so I really shouldn't be complaining. I just... I guess I haven't really bounced back to my normal self since then. \n\nI hope it's okay that I waited this long to come in. I probably should have handled it better and come to see you sooner, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any decrease in pleasure from activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I know I mentioned this a little bit already, and I hope I'm not repeating myself too much... but yeah, I guess that's exactly it. I just... I don't get that same spark from things anymore. \n\nLike I said, my fishing gear is just gathering dust out there, and the woodshop... well, I used to love spending hours in there, but now I just look at it and feel tired. And the chess with the kids... I mean, they're great kids, they really are, and I feel awful saying this, but I'm just faking it for them. I'm not actually enjoying it. I just sit there and move the pieces. \n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just feel so bad that I can't even enjoy spending time with my own family right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep hasn't been the greatest, I suppose. I mean, I know everyone has trouble sleeping sometimes, especially when they're busy, so I'm sure it's nothing too out of the ordinary. \n\nBut, uh, I can get into bed just completely exhausted after a long day at the site, and... I either just lie there running through all the bills and work schedules in my head, or I'll fall asleep and then wake up around three or four in the morning. And then I just kind of stare at the ceiling until it's time to drive to work. \n\nI'm probably just letting the stress get to me too much, right? I know I should be managing it better so I can be well-rested for my family and my crew. I hope that's the kind of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about things that you wouldn't normally feel that way about?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I know I shouldn't think this way, and I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself. I know I have a lot to be thankful for. But... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty awful about myself lately. \n\nIt just feels like I'm letting everyone down. Like I've failed my wife by not being the rock she needs right now, and I'm failing the kids because I'm just going through the motions with them. And at work... I don't know, it's like every little thing that goes wrong on the site—if we go over budget on materials, or if someone misses a shift, or even just a minor safety hiccup—I just take it so personally. I keep thinking it's all my fault, like it's proof that I'm completely useless. \n\nSometimes I even catch myself thinking that they'd all just be better off if I wasn't the one in charge anymore. I know that's probably a terrible thing to say, and I really should be stronger than this. I'm trying to push through it, I promise. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting to normal work stress.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I mean... I know that's a really important question, and I want to be completely honest with you. I don't want you to think I'm crazy or anything, or that I'm, you know, going to do something terrible. I know I have a family depending on me. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, on those early drives to the site before anyone else is awake... it's just so quiet, and I'm so tired. I catch myself thinking that maybe it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. Like if something just happened. \n\nAnd sometimes... My wife made my favorite roast last week and I barely touched it, which made me feel terrible because she worked hard on it. \n\nAnd, well, I guess my belt has had to come in a couple of notches recently, even though I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Oh, um... I'm sorry, I know we kind of just touched on that a second ago, too. I really hope I'm not frustrating you, I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need. 

But, uh, no... no trouble sitting still. Honestly, once I get home from the site and sit down in my chair, I pretty much just stay put for the rest of the night. I definitely don't feel jittery or like I need to pace around or anything like that. I mostly just try to stay quiet and rest up so I can be ready and focused for the guys the next day.

Um, well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or anything, because I know a lot of people have it worse. But, uh... no, not really.

I hope it's okay to say that? I don't want to sound like I'm not trying to get better or anything, I just... I haven't really had a couple of good months in a row since all that started. Is that the kind of timeline you were looking for?

But... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty awful about myself lately. \n\nIt just feels like I'm letting everyone down. Like I've failed my wife by not being the rock she needs right now, and I'm failing the kids because I'm just going through the motions with them. And at work... I don't know, it's like every little thing that goes wrong on the site—if we go over budget on materials, or if someone misses a shift, or even just a minor safety hiccup—I just take it so personally. I hope this doesn't sound too awful, but sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me, because it feels a little too close, you know? And the chess with the kids... I mean, they're great kids, they really are, and I feel awful saying this, but I'm just faking it for them. I'm not actually enjoying it. I just sit there and move the pieces. \n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean? I hope I'm giving you the right information. I just feel so bad that I can't even enjoy spending time with my own family right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my sleep hasn't been the greatest, I suppose. I mean, I know everyone has trouble sleeping sometimes, especially when they're busy, so I'm sure it's nothing too out of the ordinary. \n\nBut, uh, I can get into bed just completely exhausted after a long day at the site, and... I either just lie there running through all the bills and work schedules in my head, or I'll fall asleep and then wake up around three or four in the morning. And then I just kind of stare at the ceiling until it's time to drive to work. \n\nI'm probably just letting the stress get to me too much, right? I know I should be managing it better so I can be well-rested for my family and my crew. I hope that's the kind of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about things that you wouldn't normally feel that way about?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I know I shouldn't think this way, and I really hope this doesn't make me sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself. I know I have a lot to be thankful for. But... yeah, I guess I have been feeling pretty awful about myself lately. \n\nIt just feels like I'm letting everyone down. Like I've failed my wife by not being the rock she needs right now, and I'm failing the kids because I'm just going through the motions with them. And at work... I don't know, it's like every little thing that goes wrong on the site—if we go over budget on materials, or if someone misses a shift, or even just a minor safety hiccup—I just take it so personally. I keep thinking it's all my fault, like it's proof that I'm completely useless. \n\nSometimes I even catch myself thinking that they'd all just be better off if I wasn't the one in charge anymore. I know that's probably a terrible thing to say, and I really should be stronger than this. I'm trying to push through it, I promise. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting to normal work stress.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or dying lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I mean... I catch myself thinking that maybe it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. Like if something just happened. \n\nAnd sometimes... I hope this doesn't sound too awful, but sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me, because it feels a little too close, you know? Like the thought is just sitting right there. \n\nI would never want to put my family through that, I promise. I know it's a terrible way to think. I'm just so exhausted. Does that... I mean, is that something people with stress go through? I hope I'm not alarming you too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Well, um... It's almost like chewing on cardboard most days. So I guess I have been skipping breakfast before heading to the site, and even when I get home after a long shift, I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife made my favorite roast last week and I barely touched it, which made me feel terrible because she worked hard on it. \n\nAnd, well, I guess my belt has had to come in a couple of notches recently, even though I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything. I just try to stay as still and quiet as I can so I don't bother my wife while she's resting. \n\nI hope it's okay that my answer is basically the same as before? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I, uh, I hope I'm not making it sound like I don't take care of myself. I know how important it is to keep my strength up, especially being out on the job sites and needing to help out around the house. I really do try to eat right, I promise.

But, um... I guess I have lost a little bit of weight. Not on purpose, really. It's just... lately, most days, food just kind of tastes like cardboard to me. So I'll, you know, I'll end up skipping breakfast, and then I just sort of pick at my dinner, even if I've been working hard all day. I just don't have the appetite for it. I did notice I had to bring my belt in a couple of notches recently, so I guess I have dropped some pounds. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound irresponsible or like I'm not trying to stay healthy. I just... eating feels like such a chore right now. Is that... is that the kind of thing you need to know? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right details.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I really try my best to keep up, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm slacking off at work or leaving my wife to handle everything at home. I know I have to pull my weight, and I really do push myself to get things done.

But, um... I guess if I'm being totally honest, it has felt a little harder lately. I wouldn't say I'm moving in slow motion or anything dramatic like that. It's just... maybe it takes me a little longer to get going in the morning, or I feel a bit more sluggish when I'm walking the job site. I try really hard to hide it so the guys don't notice, because I need to set a good example for them. 

I'm still getting my work done, though. I make sure of that. I just... I guess I feel a little weighed down, maybe? I hope that doesn't make me sound lazy. I really am trying my best to stay on top of things. Is that... does that answer what you were asking?

I hope this doesn't sound too awful, but sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me, because it feels a little too close, you know? Like the thought is just sitting right there. \n\nI would never want to put my family through that, I promise. I know it's a terrible way to think. I'm just so exhausted. Does that... I mean, is that something people with stress go through? I hope I'm not alarming you too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Like I've failed my wife by not being the rock she needs right now, and I'm failing the kids because I'm just going through the motions with them. And at work... I don't know, it's like every little thing that goes wrong on the site—if we go over budget on materials, or if someone misses a shift, or even just a minor safety hiccup—I just take it so personally. I keep thinking it's all my fault, like it's proof that I'm completely useless. \n\nSometimes I even catch myself thinking that they'd all just be better off if I wasn't the one in charge anymore. I don't want you to think I'm just starving myself on purpose or anything like that. \n\nBut, uh, lately... I don't know, it's just like food doesn't really have any taste anymore? It's almost like chewing on cardboard most days. So I guess I have been skipping breakfast before heading to the site, and even when I get home after a long shift, I just kind of pick at my dinner. My wife made my favorite roast last week and I barely touched it, which made me feel terrible because she worked hard on it. \n\nAnd, well, I guess my belt has had to come in a couple of notches recently, even though I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything. I'm sure it's just the stress of the job, right? Like my body is just running on adrenaline? I hope it's okay that I haven't been eating as much as I should be. I don't have that nervous energy where I need to be moving around or tapping my foot or anything. I just try to stay as still and quiet as I can so I don't bother my wife while she's resting. \n\nI hope it's okay that my answer is basically the same as before? I just want to be completely honest with you and make sure I'm giving you the right information. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than others or having trouble keeping up with your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try my best to keep up, I promise. Um, well... I really try not to let things slip, you know? I've always prided myself on being reliable. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it has been getting harder.
